The LP can be interpreted: Given f and bound M, maximize sum g such that (f*g)_k ≤ M. f,g nonnegative. This is a packing problem. The dual: minimize M∑ y_k s.t. ∑_{k: pair i in conv} f_i y_k ≥1? More precisely constraint C_i = ∑_{j} f_j g_{i-j} ≤ M for k. Variables g_j. Dual variables y_k≥0 for each constraint. LP: primal max 1^T g, s.t. for all k, ∑_j K_{k,j} g_j ≤ M, g≥0, where K_{k,j}=f_{k-j?} if valid. Dual min M∑ y_k s.t. for each j, ∑_k K_{k,j} y_k ≥1, y≥0. By total unimodularity? Not. Complementary slackness: at optimum, y_k>0 only where constraint tight. We can solve dual perhaps easier? K_{k,j}=f_{k-j} for k-j in [0,n-1]. So dual constraint for g_j: ∑_{k} f_{k-j} y_k ≥1. Let's design helper functions:

`_next_pow2(m)`: return 1 << (m-1).bit_length().

`fast_full_conv(a)`:
```
def fast_full_conv(a):
    n = a.size
    m = 2*n -1
    M = 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, M)[:m]
    return c
```
Potential memory for n=131072: M=262144, A complex 262144*16=4MB, okay. n=262144: M=524288, ~8MB; maybe okay but time. Limit max length maybe 262144.

`exact_score(c,a)` maybe.
```
def exact_score(a):
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-12: return np.inf
    c = fast_full_conv(a)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    M = float(c.max())
    return float(2.0*a.size*M/(S*S))
```
Small negative due FFT set to 0.

`lse_score_and_grad(a,beta)`:
Need stable.
```
def lse_score_and_grad(a, beta):
    n=a.size; S=float(a.sum())
    if S<1e-12: return 1e30, np.zeros_like(a)
    c=fast_full_conv(a)
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    cmax=float(c.max())
    scaled=beta*(c-cmax)
    # clip lower to -745 to avoid underflow? exp handles underflow to 0, okay but log(0) if all underflow? cmax term exp(0)=1 so sum≥1.
    w=np.exp(scaled)
    wsum=w.sum()
    logsum=np.log(wsum)
    L=cmax + logsum/beta
    score=2.0*n*L/(S*S)
    p=w/wsum
    # grad L
    r=a[::-1]
    M2=3*n - 2? Need conv length of r (n) and p (2n-1) =3n-2. Previous used 3n-2.
    M2 = 1 << (M2-1).bit_length()
    R=np.fft.rfft(r, M2)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p, M2)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(R*P, M2)[n-1:2*n-1]
    g=2.0*conv
    S2=S*S; S3=S2*S
    grad=(2.0*n)*(g/S2 - 2.0*L/S3)
    # Ensure no NaN
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)): grad=np.zeros_like(a)
    return score, grad
```
Need verify for p length 2n-1, `M2 = 3n-2` correct. Suppose n=3, r len3 p len5 conv len7=3n-2. Slice [2:5] length3. Good.

`upsample(a, new_n)`: area-preserving interpolation.
```
def resample_sequence(a, new_n):
    if len(a)==new_n: return a.copy()
    old_n=len(a)
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,old_n,endpoint=False) # centers? maybe [0.5/(old), 1-0.5/old]
    x_new=np.linspace(0,1,new_n,endpoint=False)
    # use linear interpolation of heights
    b=np.interp(x_new, x_old, a, period=None, left=0,right=0)
    S=a.sum()
    # preserve sum approximately: scale b to S
    if b.sum()>0: b *= S/b.sum()
    else: b=np.ones(new_n)*S/new_n
    return b
```
But if a has sharp patterns, interp may smooth; okay. Could also do `np.repeat` if ratio integer and then box filter. For high quality, use `np.interp` of cumulative? Let's test all ones: b uniform. Good. For alternating high-low, interp smooths; maybe okay. Maybe use piecewise constant area: `idx = (np.arange(new_n)*old_n/new_n).astype(int); b = a[idx % old_n]`; then scale. This preserves pattern but jagged. Could blend: b = 0.5*interp +0.5*nearest. We'll implement `resample_sequence` with optional mode: linear if new_n> old_n else average pooling. For downsampling, use cumulative area:
```
def resample_sequence(a, new_n):
  old_n=a.size
  if new_n>old_n:
    # use mapping new->old fractional center
    u=(np.arange(new_n)+0.5)*old_n/new_n -0.5
    i0=floor(u).clip(0,old_n-1); i1=min(i0+1,old_n-1); f=u-i0
    b=(1-f)*a[i0]+f*a[i1]
  else:
    # average old per bin: use np.add.reduceat? Simpler: idx old_to_new = floor(np.arange(old_n)*new_n/old_n); b[idx]+=a; counts; normalize per bin
  scale to sum(a)
```

def refine_sequence(a0, beta_schedule, deadline, label, rng=None, max_iter_per_beta=2000, maxfun_per_beta=50000):
    best_a=a0.copy(); best_sc=exact_score(a0)
    for beta in beta_schedule:
      if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
      sc, a_opt, res = run_lbfgs_a(normalize_sum(a0,1.0), beta, maxiter=..., deadline=deadline)
      sc2=exact_score(a_opt)
      if sc2<best_sc: best_a=a_opt.copy(); best_sc=sc2
      a0=a_opt
    return best_sc,best_a
```

Let's design inits generation top-level:
```
def build_candidate_inits(n, rng, parent, best_low, max_inits=20):
    inits=[]
    # baselines
    inits.append(np.ones(n)*0.5)
    if parent is not None: inits.append(resample_sequence(parent,n))
    if best_low is not None: inits.append(resample_sequence(best_low,n))
    x = np.arange(n)/n
    for amp in [0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7]:
       inits.append(np.clip(0.5 + amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*x),0, None))
       inits.append(np.clip(0.5 + amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*x),0,None))
    # multi Fourier
    for trial in range(6):
       b=np.ones(n)
       for m in range(1,6):
          freq=int(rng.integers(1, max(2,n//10+1)))
          phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
          coeff=0.15/rng.uniform(0.5,1.5)
          b += coeff*np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*x+phase)
       inits.append(np.clip(b,0.01,None))
    # chirps
    for trial in range(4):
       q=rng.uniform(2,20)
       phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
       b=1 + 0.6*np.cos(2*np.pi*(q*x**2+phase))
       inits.append(np.clip(b,0.01,None))
    # sparse/Bernoulli
    for dens in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2]:
       mask=rng.random(n)<dens
       b=np.where(mask, 1.0, 0.05)
       # random weights
       b = b * np.exp(0.5*rng.standard_normal(n))
       inits.append(np.clip(b,1e-4,None))
    # random smooth
    for sigma in [0.05,0.1,0.2]:
       b=0.2+0.8*(1+sigma*rng.standard_normal(n))
       # smooth via FFT lowpass? simple gaussian kernel? use np.convolve small? For n large, use FFT? Maybe skip.
       inits.append(np.clip(b,1e-4,None))
    # normalize all to sum 1 and cap max 1000
    inits = [normalize_sum(np.clip(a,0,1000),1.0) if a.sum()>0 else np.ones(n)/n for a in inits]
    # unique? not necessary
    return inits
```

`lbfgs_objective(x, beta)`:
```
def lbfgs_objective(x, beta):
    # x can be 1d array
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return 1e30, np.zeros_like(x)
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    # tiny negative clip? maybe if negative beyond -1e-8 set 0
    if np.any(x < -1e-8):
       x = np.maximum(x,0.0)
    S=x.sum()
    if S < 1e-10:
       x = np.ones_like(x)/x.size
    sc, gr = lse_score_and_grad(x, beta)
    if not np.isfinite(sc): sc=1e30; gr=np.zeros_like(x)
    # clean tiny negative grad? no
    return sc, gr
```
If x has very small sum, objective finite? scale invariant, sum zero bad. Use uniform.

`run_lbfgs(a0, beta, maxiter, maxfun, deadline=None)`:
```
def run_lbfgs(a0, beta, maxiter=1000, maxfun=20000):
    a0=np.clip(a0,0,1000).astype(float)
    if a0.sum()<1e-10: a0=np.ones_like(a0)/a0.size
    bounds=[(0.0,1000.0)] for n in (0.0,1000.0)
    res=minimize(lbfgs_objective, a0, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options={...})
    a_out=np.clip(res.x,0,1000)
    if sum small: ...
    return exact_score(a_out), a_out, res.success
```

Now, improve initializations via `apply_random_smooth_perturb(a, rng, scale)`: Add Fourier random low-pass to a and clip.
```
def add_random_fourier_perturb(a, rng, n_harm=10, amp=0.1):
   n=a.size; x=np.arange(n)/n
   d=np.zeros(n)
   for m in range(1,n_harm+1):
      freq=int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//4+1)))
      phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi); coeff=amp/(m+1)*rng.uniform(0.5,1.5)
      d += coeff*np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*x+phase)
   b=a*(1+d)
   b=np.clip(b,0,1000)
   return b
```

`active_polish` top-level:
```
def active_polish(a, deadline, max_iters=200):
   a=normalize_sum(a,1)
   best=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score(a)
   prev_sc=best_sc
   for it in range(max_iters):
      if time>deadline: break
      c=fast_full_conv(a); M=c.max()
      # active weights: top by smooth beta very high
      beta=1000
      scaled=beta*(c-M); w=np.exp(scaled); wsum=w.sum(); p=w/wsum
      # if active count huge? use direct if nnz small else FFT gradient
      g = conv_weighted_grad(a,p) # returns 2*∑ p_k a_{k-j}
      S=1 (or compute)
      S2=S*S
      grad = (2.0*n)*(g/S2 - 2.0*M/S2) if S=1? formula 2M/S^3 *1 -> if S=1: 2M
      d = -grad
      # remove scale direction a
      aa = float(a@a)
      if aa>0: d -= (d@a)/aa * a
      # maybe also enforce nonneg by line search max_alpha
      max_alpha = np.inf
      neg = d < -1e-16
      if np.any(neg): max_alpha = min(max_alpha, np.min(-a[neg]/d[neg]))
      alpha = min(0.5, 0.1/(float(np.linalg.norm(d))+1e-12), max_alpha*0.9 if finite else 0.001)
      for trial in range(20):
          cand = a + alpha*d
          if np.all(cand>=-1e-12):
             cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
             sc=exact_score(cand)
             if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                a=cand; best_a=cand; best_sc=sc; break
          alpha*=0.5
      # optional if no improvement break after many
   return best_sc,best_a
```
But `conv_weighted_grad` with p length 2n-1. Use direct/FFT. We already have `grad_L_from_p(a,p)` returning g=2*... not including score factor. We'll define `conv_derivative_weighted(a,p)`:
```
def conv_derivative_weighted(a,p):
   n=a.size
   npz = int(np.count_nonzero(p > 1e-300))
   if npz ==0: return zeros
   if npz * n < 5_000_000 or npz < 2048:
      g=np.zeros(n)
      idx=np.nonzero(p)[0]
      for k in idx:
         pk=p[k]
         if pk<=0: continue
         j0=max(0,k-n+1); j1=min(k,n-1)
         if j1>=j0:
            seg = a[k-j1 : k-j0+1][::-1]
            g[j0:j1+1] += 2.0*pk*seg
      return g
   else:
      # FFT as lse
```

def load_parent():
   try:
      g=globals()
      if 'height_sequence_1' in g and g['height_sequence_1'] is not None:
          p=np.asarray(g['height_sequence_1'], dtype=np.float64).ravel()
          p=np.clip(p,0,1000)
          if p.size>=32 and p.sum()>1e-6: return p
   except Exception:
      pass
   return None
```

`normalize_sum(a, target=1.0)`:
```
def normalize_sum(a, target=1.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S<=1e-12:
       return np.full(a.size, target/max(1,a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    out=target*a/S
    # after scaling maybe max >1000? if target=1, max<=1. fine.
    return out
```

`exact_score` uses `fast_full_conv`. For small lengths maybe direct? FFT okay.

But in `lse_score_and_grad`, we can just use FFT always for simplicity. For high beta, direct saves. Let's share:
```
def weighted_conv_derivative(a,p):
    n=a.size
    nnz = int(np.count_nonzero(p > 1e-300))
    if nnz == 0: return np.zeros(n)
    direct_threshold = max(1, int(2_000_000 / max(1, n)))
    if nnz <= direct_threshold:
       ...
    else:
       M2=3*n-2; M2_fft=1 << (M2-1).bit_length()
       R=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], M2_fft); P=np.fft.rfft(p, M2_fft)
       return 2.0*np.fft.irfft(R*P, M2_fft)[n-1:2*n-1]
```
For p with exact zeros but many tiny due exp underflow? `p > 1e-300` okay.

`lse_score_and_grad` uses this.

`run_lbfgs` code:
```
def run_lbfgs(a0, beta, maxiter, maxfun, deadline):
    a = normalize_sum(np.clip(a0,0,1000),1.0)
    current_sc = exact_score(a)
    if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 2:
        return current_sc, a
    t = time.monotonic()
    try:
        sc_test, gr_test = lbfgs_objective(a,beta)
    except Exception:
        return current_sc, a
    t_call = max(1e-4, time.monotonic()-t)
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining <= 5: return current_sc,a
    maxfun_use = int(min(maxfun, max(80, remaining / max(0.02, 4.0*t_call))))
    maxfun_use = max(80, min(maxfun_use, 30000))
    maxiter_use = int(min(maxiter, max(40, maxfun_use//2)))
    bounds = [(0.0,1000.0)] * a.size
    try:
        res = minimize(lbfgs_objective, a, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds,
                       options={'ftol':1e-18,'gtol':1e-11,'maxiter':maxiter_use,'maxfun':maxfun_use,'maxls':20})
    except Exception:
        return current_sc,a
    a_out = np.clip(res.x,0,1000)
    if a_out.sum()<=1e-10: a_out=a.copy()
    else: a_out=normalize_sum(a_out,1.0)
    sc_out=exact_score(a_out)
    if not np.isfinite(sc_out): sc_out=current_sc; a_out=a
    return sc_out,a_out
```
Note: Running `lbfgs_objective` plus `exact_score` duplicates, but fine. `lbfgs_objective` returns LSE score not exact. `t_call` okay.

In options, `ftol=1e-18` maybe too strict causing many evals; maxfun limits. Use `gtol=1e-10`.

Potential issue: `maxfun_use` based on remaining / (4*t_call) counts function calls. L-BFGS may call line search many per iteration; okay. But if many candidates, remaining shrinks. Good.

`add_random_fourier_perturb` top-level:
```
def random_fourier_perturb(a, rng, scale=0.2, n_harm=8):
    n=a.size; x=np.arange(n)/n
    d=np.zeros(n)
    max_freq=max(2,n//4)
    for m in range(n_harm):
        freq=int(rng.integers(1, max_freq+1))
        phase=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
        amp=float(scale * rng.uniform(0.3,1.2) / (m+1))
        d += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*x + phase)
    b=a*(1.0+d)
    return np.clip(b,0,1000)
```

`resample_sequence` should handle parent/best length maybe larger than target (downsample). Implement downsample by cumulative sum bins? Let's define robust:
```
def resample_sequence(a, new_n):
    a=np.clip(np.asarray(a,float).ravel(),0,1000)
    old_n=a.size
    if old_n==0 or new_n<=0: return np.ones(1)
    if old_n==new_n: return a.copy()
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S<=0: return np.full(new_n,1.0/new_n)
    if new_n > old_n:
        u=(np.arange(new_n)+0.5)*old_n/new_n - 0.5
        i0=np.floor(u).astype(int); i0=np.clip(i0,0,old_n-1)
        i1=np.clip(i0+1,0,old_n-1)
        f=np.clip(u - i0,0,1)
        b=(1-f)*a[i0]+f*a[i1]
    else:
        # average over bins by assigning old samples to new bins
        idx=(np.arange(old_n)*new_n/old_n).astype(int)
        idx=np.clip(idx,0,new_n-1)
        b=np.zeros(new_n)
        counts=np.zeros(new_n)
        for? vectorized np.add.at:
        np.add.at(b, idx, a)
        np.add.at(counts, idx, 1.0)
        b[np.counts>0] /= counts[counts>0]
    # area-preserving scaling: The interpolation isn't exactly sum S; normalize to S
    sb=float(b.sum())
    if sb>0: b *= S/sb
    return np.clip(b,0,1000)
```
`np.add.at` okay. This downsample averages, not sum; then scale to S. For old_n multiple new, each bin exactly count, values average; sum scaled to S. Good.

For upsampling nearest/linear, sum scaled to S. It changes n, so scale invariant; good.

Potential issue: If sequence has zeros, resample smooths zeros to small positive; okay.

Now, final local perturbations. Define `targeted_perturb(a, rng, strength)`:
```
def targeted_perturb(a, rng, strength=0.05):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).copy()
    n=a.size
    c=fast_full_conv(a)
    # active peaks top 3
    order=np.argsort(c)[::-1]
    b=a.copy()
    for _ in range(3):
       k=int(order[_]) if len(order)>0 else n//2
       j0=max(0,k-n+1); j1=min(k,n-1)
       if j1<j0: continue
       contrib = a[j0:j1+1][::-1] # derivative/2
       total=b.sum()
       top_count=max(5, n//200)
       # local top within contrib segment? We want reduce elements j where contrib large.
       # Get indices segment
       idx_seg = np.arange(j0,j1+1)
       order_idx = idx_seg[np.argsort(contrib, kind='stable')[::-1]]
       reduce_idx = order_idx[:top_count]
       # add to segment? or globally low contrib
       # reduce amount proportional to b[j]*strength capped 0.5
       reduced=0
       for j in reduce_idx:
          amt = min(b[j]*strength, b[j]*0.5)
          b[j]-=amt; reduced+=amt
       if reduced>0:
          # add to bottom third of segment or globally random not in reduce
          add_idx = idx_seg[np.argsort(contrib)[:top_count]]
          # avoid overlap? if same, choose random outside
          if len(add_idx)<top_count:
             add_idx = np.concatenate([add_idx, rng.choice(n,top_count,replace=False)])
          per=reduced/len(add_idx)
          for j in add_idx: b[j]+=per
    b=np.clip(b,0,1000)
    if b.sum()<=0: b=a.copy()
    return normalize_sum(b,1.0)
```
Python loops over top_count (~n/200=33) *3 okay. But if called many, maybe. Use vectorized for this. Simpler:
```
contrib_full = np.zeros(n)
if segment: contrib_full[j0:j1+1]=a[j0:j1+1][::-1]
top_idx=np.argsort(contrib_full)[::-1][:top_count]
bot_idx=np.argsort(contrib_full)[:top_count]
reduce = b[top_idx]*strength; b[top_idx]-=reduce; reduced=sum(reduce)
b[bot_idx]+=reduced/bot_idx.size
```
Do repeat with active peaks. Use same contrib from actual a not b? okay. This targeted moves.

Implement `generate_lp_direction(a_low, M=None)`:
```
def generate_lp_direction(f):
    n=f.size
    if n<64 or n>1024: 
       if n>1024: f=resample_sequence(f,1024)
    f=normalize_sum(np.clip(f,0,1000),1.0)
    f=np.maximum(f, 1e-8)
    c=fast_full_conv(f); M=float(c.max())
    if M<=0: return None
    P = np.zeros((2*n-1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    # fill: for j in range(n): P[j:j+n, j] = f
    for j in range(n):
        P[j:j+n, j] = f
    # To limit LP size maybe n=512. If n=1024, P 2047x1024 ~2M, okay but time.
    b_ub = np.full(2*n-1, M)
    c_obj = -np.ones(n) # minimize -sum
    try:
       res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=P, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=[(0,10.0)]*n, method='highs', options={'time_limit': 5.0?})
       if res.status==0 and res.x is not None and res.x.sum()>0:
           return np.clip(res.x,0,100)
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
`linprog` options for HiGHS time_limit? For highspy, options={'time_limit':5.0}. But different solvers? Use default; n small. Set `options={'presolve': True}`. To avoid long, maybe not.
LP with P dense may be slow for 1024; use n_low=256/512. State-of-art maybe low. We'll call with low_best resampled to 256 or 512. If f positive min 1e-8, M maybe ~0.002? capacity small; variables capped10. LP might find g. But building P loop 512*1023 ~0.5M, okay. HiGHS can solve 512 vars, 1023 constraints in <1s.

If LP direction g, update high candidate:
```

def consider(a, score, best_a, best_sc, label):
    if a is None:
        return best_a, best_sc
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(score):
        return best_a, best_sc
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if a.sum() <= 1e-12:
        return best_a, best_sc
    if score < best_sc:
        best_sc = float(score)
        best_a = a.copy()
        print(f"[{label}] new best internal={best_sc:.10f} n={best_a.size} t={time.monotonic()-PROPOSAL_START:.1f}s")
    return best_a, best_sc

`build_candidate_inits` uses best_prev maybe None. It needs produce unique-ish. We can limit total. Let's implement:
```
def build_candidate_inits(n, rng, parent, best_prev, max_inits=14):
    inits=[]
    n=int(n)
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)/float(n)
    # uniform
    inits.append(np.full(n,1.0))
    if parent is not None and parent.size>0:
        inits.append(resample_sequence(parent,n))
    if best_prev is not None and best_prev.size>0:
        inits.append(resample_sequence(best_prev,n))
        inits.append(random_fourier_perturb(resample_sequence(best_prev,n), rng, 0.15, 10))
        inits.append(random_fourier_perturb(resample_sequence(best_prev,n), rng, 0.30, 20))
    # sinusoidal
    for freq in [1,2,3,5,8]:
        for amp in [0.2,0.5,0.8]:
            b=1.0 + amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*x)
            inits.append(np.maximum(b,1e-6))
            b=1.0 + amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*freq*x)
            inits.append(np.maximum(b,1e-6))
    if len(inits) < max_inits + 6:
       for _ in range(6):
          inits.append(fourier_init(n,rng,n_harm=8))
       for _ in range(4):
          inits.append(chirp_init(n,rng))
       for dens in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2]:
          inits.append(sparse_init(n,rng,dens))
       inits.append(np.clip(1.0+0.4*rng.standard_normal(n),1e-6,1000))
       inits.append(np.clip(1.0+0.8*rng.standard_normal(n),1e-6,1000))
    # normalize and drop bad, cap
    out=[]
    for a in inits:
       if len(out)>=max_inits: break
       b=np.clip(np.asarray(a,float),0,1000)
       if b.size!=n:
          b=resample_sequence(b,n)
       b=normalize_sum(b,1.0)
       if b.size==n and b.sum()>1e-12:
          out.append(b)
    return out
```
This may exceed max_inits due to sinusoidals 30 before cap. It caps at max_inits, so later variants ignored. If we want diversity, max_inits maybe 20 and sinusoid variations included. For high n, fewer. Good.

`make_perturbed` top-level:
```
def make_perturbed(a, rng, strategy):
   a=normalize_sum(a,1.0)
   if strategy==0:
      scale=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.2))
      b=a*(1+scale*rng.standard_normal(a.size))
   elif strategy==1:
      b=targeted_perturb(a, rng, strength=float(rng.uniform(0.03,0.12)))
   elif strategy==2:
      b=random_fourier_perturb(a, rng, scale=float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.35)), n_harm=12)
   else:
      b=a.copy()
      # block swap: choose segment and reverse/scale
      n=b.size; w=max(5,n//20)
      start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-w)))
      seg=b[start:start+w].copy()
      b[start:start+w] = np.maximum(seg,0) * float(rng.uniform(0.5,1.5))
   return normalize_sum(np.clip(b,0,1000),1.0)
```
Need ensure `start+w<=n`: use `max(1, n-w)` and if n-w<=0 skip.

`targeted_perturb`: Use exact conv and active peaks. Vectorized as described.
```
def targeted_perturb(a, rng, strength=0.05):
    a=normalize_sum(a,1.0)
    n=a.size
    c=fast_full_conv(a)
    b=a.copy()
    top_count=max(8, n//200)
    # choose up to 4 active peaks
    order=np.argsort(c)[::-1]
    for kk in order[:4]:
       k=int(kk)
       j0=max(0,k-n+1); j1=min(k,n-1)
       if j1<j0: continue
       contrib=np.zeros(n)
       contrib[j0:j1+1] = a[j0:j1+1][::-1]
       # top and bottom by contribution
       top_idx=np.argsort(contrib, kind='stable')[::-1][:top_count]
       bot_idx=np.argsort(contrib, kind='stable')[:top_count]
       red_amt = b[top_idx] * strength
       # cap at half current
       red_amt = np.minimum(red_amt, 0.4*b[top_idx])
       b[top_idx] -= red_amt
       reduced=float(red_amt.sum())
       if reduced>0 and bot_idx.size>0:
          b[bot_idx] += reduced / float(bot_idx.size)
       # if some top got zero, no issue
       if not np.any(b < 0): pass
       b = np.clip(b,0,1000)
    if b.sum()<=0: b=a.copy()
    return normalize_sum(b,1.0)
```
`np.argsort(contrib, kind='stable')` computes full n sort; for n=16k, 4 times ~ okay. Could be slow if many trials; okay.

Potential issue: `b[top_idx]` after previous peaks; contrib from original a. Fine.

`gradient_line_search`:
```
def gradient_line_search(a, beta, max_evals, deadline):
    a=normalize_sum(a,1.0)
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score(a)
    evals=0
    for it in range(max_evals):
       if time.monotonic()>deadline or evals>=max_evals: break
       sc_lse, gr = lse_score_and_grad(a,beta)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(gr)): break
       d = -gr
       aa=float(a@a)
       if aa>0: d -= (d@a)/aa*a
       dn=float(np.linalg.norm(d))
       if dn < 1e-14: break
       d = d/dn
       max_alpha=1.0
       neg = d < -1e-12
       if np.any(neg):
          vals = -a[neg]/d[neg]
          max_alpha=min(max_alpha, float(np.min(vals)))
       if max_alpha <= 0:
          # try small positive where d positive? cannot increase if would violate? Use d clipped?
          alpha=0.0
       else:
          alpha=min(0.2, 0.5*max_alpha)
       if alpha<=0: break
       improved=False
       for _ in range(8):
          cand = a + alpha*d
          if np.all(cand >= -1e-10):
             cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
             evals+=1
             sc=exact_score(cand)
             if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                a=cand; best_a=cand; best_sc=sc; improved=True; break
          alpha*=0.5
          if alpha<1e-8: break
       if not improved and evals>=max_evals: break
    return best_sc,best_a
```
But if d has negative components, max_alpha finite. If d all positive, max_alpha=1; increasing all keeps nonneg; but removing scale direction may make mixed. Good. It uses LSE gradient with beta; we don't use exact subgradient. Fine.

Maybe line search too slow; max_evals=50. Use only if remaining >100s.

Implement golden section top-level:
```
def golden_minimize(func, lo, hi, iters=18):
    gr=(1+math.sqrt(5))/2
    c = hi - (hi-lo)/gr
    d = lo + (hi-lo)/gr
    fc=func(c); fd=func(d)
    for _ in range(iters):
       if fc < fd:
          hi=d; d=c; fd=fc; c=hi-(hi-lo)/gr; fc=func(c)
       else:
          lo=c; c=d; fc=fd; d=lo+(hi-lo)/gr; fd=func(d)
    return (lo+hi)/2
```
Need func closure? We can't use closures. We can pass a top-level function that takes value, but needs params. Without closures, difficult. def optimize_fourier_param(params, idx, lo, hi, n, iters=16):
   gr = (1+math.sqrt(5))/2
   c = hi - (hi-lo)/gr
   d = lo + (hi-lo)/gr
   fc = fourier_score_value(params, idx, c, n)

```
def optimize_fourier_param(params, idx, lo, hi, orders, n, iters=12):
    params = params.copy()
    gr = (1.0 + math.sqrt(5.0)) * 0.5
    c = hi - (hi - lo) / gr
    d = lo + (hi - lo) / gr
    fc = fourier_score_value(params, idx, float(c), orders, n)
    fd = fourier_score_value(params, idx, float(d), orders, n)
    for _ in range(iters):
       if fc < fd:
           hi = d; d = c; fd = fc; c = hi - (hi-lo)/gr; fc = fourier_score_value(params, idx, float(c), orders, n)
       else:
           lo = c; c = d; fc = fd; d = lo + (hi-lo)/gr; fd = fourier_score_value(params, idx, float(d), orders, n)
    best = 0.5*(lo+hi)
    params[idx] = float(best)
    sc = fourier_score_value(params, idx, float(best), orders, n)
    return params, sc
```

### Potential issue with L-BFGS from very sparse init: Some entries exactly 0, bounds; gradient for zeros? LSE gradient at boundary can be positive, L-BFGS can increase from zero? With bounds, if gradient points into feasible (increase), it can. Good.

### Could L-BFGS try values causing sum 1 and some >1000? If one variable 1000 and sum1 impossible with nonnegative others? Others can be 0; value ≤1 if sum1? Actually if sum1, one value can be1 max. L-BFGS may temporarily violate sum because objective scale invariant and not constrained; it can scale all values up while changing shape. Starting sum1, it could make sum large; bounds upper1000. If an entry hits1000 and others positive, sum could >1. Objective invariant but gradient not necessarily keep sum1; it might follow scale up to 1000 causing numerical. However since scale direction flat (gradient dot a=0 for smooth LSE), L-BFGS should not move along a significantly, but line search maybe. To mitigate, we can *renormalize inside objective*? That would make objective independent of scale and gradient formula with normalized a. Let's consider changing objective to normalize a to sum1 inside `lbfgs_objective`. L-BFGS then sees a on arbitrary scale but objective computed on normalized. The gradient wrt x must account for normalization; scale direction gradient becomes zero, but also L-BFGS may have flat directions. Could be okay and prevents scaling to 1000. Compute:
- Given x, y = normalize_sum(x,1) (if sum>0).
- Compute LSE score/grad wrt y.
- Gradient wrt x: if y = x/S, ∂y_j = (δ_j x - y_j S)/S^2 = (x_j/S - y_j)/x? Actually y=x/S. dy_j/dx_i = (δ_{ij} S - x_j)/S^2 = (δ_{ij}/S - y_i? wait x_j/S? Let's derive y_i=x_i/S. ∂y_i/∂x_j = (δ_{ij} S - x_i)/S^2 = (δ_{ij} - y_i S? no x_i/S = y_i, so (δ_ij - y_i)/S.
Then grad_x_j = ∑_i grad_y_i ∂y_i/∂x_j = grad_y_j/S - (y_j/S)∑_i grad_y_i.

   x=np.asarray(...)

#### `fast_full_conv` for a list? We always pass np array. If not, `np.asarray`.
```
def fast_full_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    m = 2*n - 1
    M = 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, M)
    c = c[:m]
    return c
```
If m=0? n=0 not.

#### `exact_score`
```
def exact_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-12: return np.inf
    c=fast_full_conv(np.clip(a,0,1000))
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    M=float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(M): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*a.size*M/(S*S))
```
If a contains negative, exact uses clip? The true nonnegative; for internal, use clip to match. Good.

def sparse_init(n, rng, density):
    b=np.full(n, 1e-4)
    if density <=0: return b
    mask = rng.random(n) < density
    vals = np.exp(0.5 * rng.standard_normal(int(mask.sum())) if mask.sum()>0 else 0)
    b[mask] = vals
    return np.clip(b,0,1000)
```

### Final code:

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative coefficient sequence minimizing

    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2

using a multiresolution strategy:
  - exact FFT-based convolution / LSE-smoothed max coefficient objective
  - L-BFGS-B continuation in LSE temperature
  - random Fourier / chirp / sparse / perturbations initializations
  - low-dimensional Fourier parameter golden-section search at low resolution
  - low-resolution linear-programming direction from the previous state of art
  - final high-beta refinement, exact gradient line search, and local moves
The function propose_candidate() returns the best list found within budget.
"""

import time
import math
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize, linprog
from operator import itemgetter

_T0 = time.monotonic()


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Basic helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def load_parent():
    """Load heuristic parent sequence if present as height_sequence_1."""
    try:
        g = globals()
        if 'height_sequence_1' in g and g['height_sequence_1'] is not None:
            p = np.asarray(g['height_sequence_1'], dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if p.size >= 32 and float(p.sum()) > 1e-9:
                return p
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None


def normalize_sum(a, target=1.0):
    """Clip to [0,1000] and rescale so that the sum is target (or uniform if zero)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-14:
        return np.full(a.size, max(1e-12, float(target) / float(a.size)), dtype=np.float64)
    return target * a / s


def fast_full_conv(a):
    """Full linear self-convolution via FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    m = 2 * n - 1
    M = 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, M)
    return c[:m]


def exact_score(a):
    """Exact objective for a nonnegative sequence."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-14:
        return np.inf
    c = fast_full_conv(a)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    m = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * a.size * m / (s * s))


def weighted_conv_derivative(a, p):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k p_k * (conv(a,a))_k with respect to a.
    p has length 2n-1.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    nnz = int(np.count_nonzero(p > 1e-300))
    if nnz == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    # Direct summation is faster when only a few active peak weights.
    direct_threshold = max(16, int(2_000_000 / max(1, n)))
    if nnz <= direct_threshold:
        g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        idx = np.nonzero(p > 1e-300)[0]
        for k in idx:
            pk = float(p[k])
            if pk <= 0.0:
                continue
            j0 = max(0, int(k) - n + 1)
            j1 = min(int(k), n - 1)
            if j1 >= j0:
                seg = a[int(k) - j1:int(k) - j0 + 1][::-1]
                g[j0:j1 + 1] += 2.0 * pk * seg
        return g

    # FFT method.
    r = a[::-1]
    M2 = 3 * n - 2
    M2_fft = 1 << (M2 - 1).bit_length()
    R = np.fft.rfft(r, M2_fft)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, M2_fft)
    out = np.fft.irfft(R * P, M2_fft)
    return 2.0 * out[n - 1:2 * n - 1]


def lse_score_and_grad(a, beta):
    """
    Smoothed objective using log-sum-exp over coefficients.
    Returns (score, grad).
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf, np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-14:
        a = np.full(a.size, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = 1.0

    n = a.size
    c = fast_full_conv(a)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    cmax = float(c.max())

    beta = max(1.0, float(beta))
    w = np.exp(beta * (c - cmax))
    wsum = float(w.sum())
    if wsum <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(wsum):
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        wsum = 1.0

    p = w / wsum
    L = cmax + math.log(wsum) / beta
    score = float(2.0 * n * L / (s * s))

    g = weighted_conv_derivative(a, p)
    s2 = s * s
    s3 = s2 * s
    grad = (2.0 * n) * (g / s2 - (2.0 * L) / s3)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
        grad = np.zeros_like(a)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        score = 1e30
    return score, grad


def lbfgs_objective(x, beta):
    """Objective for scipy: LSE score plus a tiny sum-penalty to control scale."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
    s = float(x.sum())
    if s <= 1e-14:
        n = max(1, x.size)
        x = np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = 1.0

    sc, gr = lse_score_and_grad(x, beta)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return 1e30, np.zeros_like(x)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(gr)):
        return 1e30, np.zeros_like(x)

    # Tiny penalty keeps the L-BFGS trajectory near sum = 1 without materially
    # changing the scale-invariant objective.
    pen = 1e-9 * (s - 1.0) * (s - 1.0)
    gp = (2.0e-9 * (s - 1.0)) * np.ones_like(x)
    return sc + pen, gr + gp


def run_lbfgs(a0, beta, maxiter, maxfun, deadline):
    """Time-boxed L-BFGS-B run for one LSE temperature."""
    a0 = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a0.size == 0:
        return np.inf, np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a = normalize_sum(a0, 1.0)
    cur_sc = exact_score(a)

    if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 3.0:
        return cur_sc, a

    t_start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        lbfgs_objective(a, beta)
        t_call = max(1e-5, time.monotonic() - t_start)
    except Exception:
        return cur_sc, a

    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining <= 5.0:
        return cur_sc, a

    maxfun_use = int(min(float(maxfun), max(80.0, remaining / max(0.02, 8.0 * t_call))))
    maxfun_use = max(80, min(maxfun_use, 30000))
    maxiter_use = int(min(float(maxiter), max(40, maxfun_use // 2)))

    bounds = [(0.0, 1000.0)] * int(a.size)
    try:
        res = minimize(
            lbfgs_objective,
            a,
            args=(beta,),
            method='L-BFGS-B',
            jac=True,
            bounds=bounds,
            options={
                'ftol': 1e-15,
                'gtol': 1e-10,
                'maxiter': maxiter_use,
                'maxfun': maxfun_use,
                'maxls': 20,
            }
        )
        x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if x.sum() <= 1e-12:
            x = a.copy()
        else:
            x = normalize_sum(x, 1.0)
        sc = exact_score(x)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            return cur_sc, a
        return float(sc), x
    except Exception:
        return cur_sc, a


def consider(a, score, best_a, best_sc, label):
    """Update best if candidate is better."""
    if a is None or not np.isfinite(score):
        return best_a, best_sc
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return best_a, best_sc
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if a.sum() <= 1e-12:
        return best_a, best_sc
    if score < best_sc:
        best_sc = float(score)
        best_a = a.copy()
        try:
            print(f"[{label}] new best internal={best_sc:.10f} n={best_a.size} "
                  f"t={time.monotonic() - _T0:.1f}s")
        except Exception:
            print(f"[{label}] new best internal={best_sc:.10f} n={best_a.size}")
    return best_a, best_sc


def score_inits(inits):
    """Score initializations and return sorted list of (score, array)."""
    out = []
    for a in inits:
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size == 0:
            continue
        a = normalize_sum(np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0), 1.0)
        sc = exact_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            out.append((float(sc), a))
    out.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
    return out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Resampling and initializers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def resample_sequence(a, new_n):
    """Area-preserving-ish resampling to a new length."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    old_n = a.size
    new_n = int(max(1, new_n))
    if old_n == 0:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / float(new_n)
    if old_n == new_n:
        return a.copy()

    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-14:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / float(new_n)

    if new_n > old_n:
        u = (np.arange(new_n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) * (old_n / float(new_n)) - 0.5
        i0 = np.floor(u).astype(int)
        i0 = np.clip(i0, 0, old_n - 1)
        i1 = np.clip(i0 + 1, 0, old_n - 1)
        f = np.clip(u - i0, 0.0, 1.0)
        b = (1.0 - f) * a[i0] + f * a[i1]
    else:
        idx = (np.arange(old_n, dtype=np.float64) * (new_n / float(old_n))).astype(int)
        idx = np.clip(idx, 0, new_n - 1)
        b = np.zeros(new_n, dtype=np.float64)
        counts = np.zeros(new_n, dtype=np.float64)
        np.add.at(b, idx, a)
        np.add.at(counts, idx, 1.0)
        pos = counts > 0.0
        b[pos] /= counts[pos]

    sb = float(b.sum())
    if sb > 1e-14:
        b *= S / sb
    else:
        b = np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) * S / float(new_n)
    return np.clip(b, 0.0, 1000.0)


def fourier_init(n, rng, n_harm=8):
    """Random multi-harmonic nonnegative initializer."""
    n = max(1, int(n))
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    b = np.full(n, 1.0, dtype=np.float64)
    pop = max(2, n // 4)
    for m in range(int(n_harm)):
        freq = int(rng.integers(1, pop + 1))
        phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.1, 0.9)) / float(m + 1)
        b += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(freq) * x + phase)
    return np.clip(b, 1e-6, 1000.0)


def chirp_init(n, rng):
    """Quadratic-phase initializer, useful for anti-autocorrelation structure."""
    n = max(1, int(n))
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    q = float(rng.uniform(1.0, 20.0))
    phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
    b = 1.0 + 0.7 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * (q * x * x + phase))
    return np.clip(b, 1e-6, 1000.0)


def sparse_init(n, rng, density):
    """Sparse weighted initializer with a tiny floor to avoid exact zeros."""
    n = max(1, int(n))
    b = np.full(n, 1e-4, dtype=np.float64)
    density = float(density)
    if density <= 0.0:
        return b
    mask = rng.random(n) < density
    num = int(mask.sum())
    if num > 0:
        vals = np.exp(0.5 * rng.standard_normal(num))
        b[mask] = vals
    return np.clip(b, 1e-8, 1000.0)


def random_fourier_perturb(a, rng, scale=0.2, n_harm=10):
    """Add a random smooth Fourier pattern multiplicatively."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    d = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    pop = max(2, n // 4)
    for m in range(int(n_harm)):
        freq = int(rng.integers(1, pop + 1))
        phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        amp = float(scale) * float(rng.uniform(0.3, 1.3)) / float(m + 1)
        d += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(freq) * x + phase)
    b = a * (1.0 + d)
    return np.clip(b, 1e-8, 1000.0)


def build_candidate_inits(n, rng, parent, best_prev, max_inits=14):
    """Build a diverse collection of normalized initializations of length n."""
    n = int(max(1, n))
    inits = []

    if parent is not None:
        pp = resample_sequence(parent, n)
        inits.append(pp)
        inits.append(random_fourier_perturb(pp, rng, 0.12, 10))

    if best_prev is not None:
        bp = resample_sequence(best_prev, n)
        inits.append(bp)
        inits.append(random_fourier_perturb(bp, rng, 0.12, 10))
        inits.append(random_fourier_perturb(bp, rng, 0.28, 20))

    inits.append(np.full(n, 1.0, dtype=np.float64))

    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)

    # A few generic smooth designs.
    if len(inits) < 2 * int(max_inits):
        for _ in range(6):
            inits.append(fourier_init(n, rng, n_harm=8))
        for _ in range(4):
            inits.append(chirp_init(n, rng))
        for dens in (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35):
            inits.append(sparse_init(n, rng, dens))

        for _ in range(6):
            freq = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n // 4 + 1)))
            amp = float(rng.uniform(0.2, 0.9))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
            b = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(freq) * x + phase)
            inits.append(np.clip(b, 1e-6, 1000.0))

            b = 1.0 + amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(freq) * x + phase)
            inits.append(np.clip(b, 1e-6, 1000.0))

        inits.append(np.clip(1.0 + 0.25 * rng.standard_normal(n), 1e-6, 1000.0))
        inits.append(np.clip(1.0 + 0.60 * rng.standard_normal(n), 1e-6, 1000.0))

    # Keep distinct lengths and cap count.
    out = []
    for a in inits:
        if len(out) >= int(max_inits):
            break
        b = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if b.size != n:
            b = resample_sequence(b, n)
        b = normalize_sum(np.clip(b, 0.0, 1000.0), 1.0)
        if b.size == n and b.sum() > 1e-12:
            out.append(b)

    if not out:
        out.append(np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64))
    return out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Low-dimensional Fourier / golden-section search
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def fourier_params_to_sequence(params, orders, n):
    """Construct sequence from [base, amp_1, phase_1, amp_2, phase_2, ...]."""
    n = int(max(1, n))
    base = max(0.05, float(params[0]))
    b = np.full(n, base, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    m = (len(params) - 1) // 2
    for i in range(m):
        f = int(orders[i])
        amp = float(params[1 + 2 * i])
        phase = float(params[2 + 2 * i])
        b += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(f) * x + phase)
    return np.maximum(b, 1e-6)


def fourier_score_value(params, idx, value, orders, n):
    """Score a single Fourier parameter while all others are fixed."""
    p = np.asarray(params, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p[idx] = float(value)
    a = fourier_params_to_sequence(p, np.asarray(orders, dtype=int), int(n))
    a = normalize_sum(a, 1.0)
    sc = exact_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return 1e30
    return float(sc)


def optimize_fourier_param(params, idx, lo, hi, orders, n, iters=12):
    """Golden-section optimize one Fourier parameter."""
    params = np.asarray(params, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    lo = float(lo)
    hi = float(hi)
    if hi <= lo:
        hi = lo + 1.0e-6
    gr = 0.5 * (1.0 + math.sqrt(5.0))
    c = hi - (hi - lo) / gr
    d = lo + (hi - lo) / gr

    fc = fourier_score_value(params, idx, c, orders, n)
    fd = fourier_score_value(params, idx, d, orders, n)
    if not np.isfinite(fc):
        fc = 1e30
    if not np.isfinite(fd):
        fd = 1e30

    for _ in range(int(iters)):
        if fc < fd:
            hi = d
            d = c
            fd = fc
            c = hi - (hi - lo) / gr
            fc = fourier_score_value(params, idx, c, orders, n)
            if not np.isfinite(fc):
                fc = 1e30
        else:
            lo = c
            c = d
            fc = fd
            d = lo + (hi - lo) / gr
            fd = fourier_score_value(params, idx, d, orders, n)
            if not np.isfinite(fd):
                fd = 1e30

    best_val = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
    params[idx] = float(best_val)
    sc = fourier_score_value(params, idx, best_val, orders, n)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        sc = 1e30
    return params, float(sc)


def fourier_search(n, rng, max_cycles=3, deadline=None):
    """
    Low-dimensional search over random Fourier orders/amplitudes/phases.
    Returns a strong low-resolution initial pattern.
    """
    n = int(max(16, n))
    if deadline is None:
        deadline = time.monotonic() + 1.0

    M = int(min(8, max(3, n // 128)))
    pop = max(M, min(n // 2, max(M, n // 8)))
    best_a = None
    best_sc = np.inf

    for cy in range(int(max_cycles)):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

        orders = rng.choice(np.arange(1, pop + 1, dtype=np.int64), size=M, replace=False)
        orders = np.sort(orders)

        params = np.zeros(1 + 2 * M, dtype=np.float64)
        params[0] = float(rng.uniform(0.5, 2.0))
        params[1::2] = rng.uniform(-1.8, 1.8, size=M)
        params[2::2] = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi, size=M)

        # Two sweeps over all parameters.
        for sweep in range(2):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            # base
            params, sc = optimize_fourier_param(params, 0, 0.2, 3.0, orders, n, iters=10)
            for m in range(M):
                idx_amp = 1 + 2 * m
                idx_ph = 2 + 2 * m
                params, sc = optimize_fourier_param(params, idx_amp, -2.0, 2.0, orders, n, iters=10)
                params, sc = optimize_fourier_param(params, idx_ph, 0.0, 2.0 * np.pi, orders, n, iters=10)
                if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                    break

        a = normalize_sum(fourier_params_to_sequence(params, orders, n), 1.0)
        sc = exact_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best_a = a.copy()
            print(f"[fourier_search] n={n} cycle={cy} score={best_sc:.10f}")

    return best_a, best_sc


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# LP direction, targeted perturbations, final polish
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def generate_lp_direction(f):
    """
    Solve the low-resolution linear program:
        max sum(g)  s.t. f*g <= M, g >=0.
    Used as a basin-hopping direction.
    """
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if f.size < 32:
        return None

    n_lp = int(np.clip(f.size, 128, 512))
    f = resample_sequence(f, n_lp)
    f = normalize_sum(f, 1.0)
    f = np.maximum(f, 1e-8)

    c = fast_full_conv(f)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if M <= 1e-14 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return None

    P = np.zeros((2 * n_lp - 1, n_lp), dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n_lp):
        P[j:j + n_lp, j] = f

    c_obj = -np.ones(n_lp, dtype=np.float64)
    b_ub = np.full(2 * n_lp - 1, M, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, 10.0)] * n_lp

    try:
        res = linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=P,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=bounds,
            method='highs'
        )
        if res.status == 0 and res.x is not None:
            g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            g = np.clip(g, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if g.sum() > 1e-12:
                return g
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None


def targeted_perturb(a, rng, strength=0.05):
    """Reduce mass in elements contributing most to the largest convolution peaks."""
    a = normalize_sum(a, 1.0)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a
    c = fast_full_conv(a)
    b = a.copy()
    top_count = max(8, n // 200)

    order = np.argsort(c, kind='stable')[::-1]
    for kk in order[:4]:
        k = int(kk)
        j0 = max(0, k - n + 1)
        j1 = min(k, n - 1)
        if j1 < j0:
            continue

        contrib = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        contrib[j0:j1 + 1] = a[j0:j1 + 1][::-1]

        top_idx = np.argsort(contrib, kind='stable')[::-1][:top_count]
        bot_idx = np.argsort(contrib, kind='stable')[:top_count]

        red = b[top_idx] * float(strength)
        red = np.minimum(red, 0.4 * np.maximum(b[top_idx], 0.0))
        b[top_idx] -= red
        reduced = float(red.sum())

        if reduced > 0.0 and bot_idx.size > 0:
            b[bot_idx] += reduced / float(bot_idx.size)

        b = np.clip(b, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if b.sum() <= 1e-12:
        b = a.copy()
    return normalize_sum(b, 1.0)


def make_perturbed(a, rng, strategy):
    """Create a perturbed candidate according to an integer strategy."""
    a = normalize_sum(a, 1.0)
    n = a.size

    if strategy == 0:
        scale = float(rng.uniform(0.04, 0.18))
        b = a * (1.0 + scale * rng.standard_normal(n))

    elif strategy == 1:
        b = targeted_perturb(a, rng, strength=float(rng.uniform(0.03, 0.12)))

    elif strategy == 2:
        b = random_fourier_perturb(a, rng, scale=float(rng.uniform(0.10, 0.35)), n_harm=16)

    else:
        b = a.copy()
        if n > 16:
            w = max(5, n // 16)
            max_start = max(1, n - w)
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max_start))
            start = min(start, n - w)
            if start + w <= n:
                seg = np.maximum(b[start:start + w], 0.0)
                seg = seg * float(rng.uniform(0.5, 1.5))
                b[start:start + w] = seg

    return normalize_sum(np.clip(b, 0.0, 1000.0), 1.0)


def gradient_line_search(a, beta, max_evals=40, deadline=None):
    """
    Final exact line-search helper using high-beta LSE gradients.
    Keeps the search close to the feasible cone and accepts only exact reductions.
    """
    if deadline is None:
        deadline = time.monotonic() + 1e-9
    a = normalize_sum(a, 1.0)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf, a
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = exact_score(a)
    evals = 0
    beta = max(100.0, float(beta))

    for _ in range(int(max_evals)):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        if evals >= int(max_evals):
            break

        try:
            _, grad = lse_score_and_grad(a, beta)
        except Exception:
            break

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
            break

        d = -grad
        aa = float(np.dot(a, a))
        if aa > 0.0:
            d -= (float(np.dot(d, a)) / aa) * a

        dn = float(np.linalg.norm(d))
        if dn < 1e-14:
            break
        d = d / dn

        max_alpha = 1.0
        neg = d < -1e-12
        if np.any(neg):
            vals = -a[neg] / d[neg]
            finite = vals[np.isfinite(vals) & (vals > 0.0)]
            if finite.size > 0:
                max_alpha = min(max_alpha, float(np.min(finite)))

        if max_alpha <= 0.0:
            break

        alpha = min(0.2, 0.5 * float(max_alpha))
        improved = False

        for _trial in range(8):
            if alpha <= 1e-10:
                break
            cand = a + alpha * d
            if np.all(cand >= -1e-10):
                cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
                evals += 1
                sc = exact_score(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                    a = cand
                    best_a = cand.copy()
                    best_sc = float(sc)
                    improved = True
                    break
            alpha *= 0.5

        if not improved and evals >= int(max_evals) - 2:
            break

    return best_sc, best_a


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Main searcher
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for the best coefficient sequence within the supplied time budget.
    """
    global _T0
    _T0 = time.monotonic()
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    total_budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = _T0 + total_budget - 18.0
    work = max(30.0, deadline - _T0)

    parent = load_parent()

    best_a = None
    best_sc = np.inf

    # Very simple baselines.
    if parent is not None:
        p = normalize_sum(parent, 1.0)
        sp = exact_score(p)
        best_a, best_sc = consider(p, sp, best_a, best_sc, "parent")
    else:
        p = None

    n_work = len(parent) if parent is not None else 4096
    n_work = int(np.clip(n_work, 512, 16384))
    uniform = np.full(n_work, 1.0 / float(n_work), dtype=np.float64)
    best_a, best_sc = consider(uniform, exact_score(uniform), best_a, best_sc, "uniform")

    if best_a is None:
        best_a = uniform.copy()
        best_sc = exact_score(best_a)

    low_best_a = None
    low_best_sc = np.inf

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 1: low-resolution exploration
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    low_deadline = _T0 + 0.20 * work
    low_lengths = [256, 512, 1024, 2048]

    for n_low in low_lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > low_deadline:
            break

        inits = build_candidate_inits(n_low, rng, parent, best_a, max_inits=14)
        scored = score_inits(inits)
        if not scored:
            continue

        sc0, a0 = scored[0]
        best_a, best_sc = consider(a0, sc0, best_a, best_sc, f"low-init-n{n_low}")
        if sc0 < low_best_sc:
            low_best_sc = float(sc0)
            low_best_a = a0.copy()

        # Fourier parameter search can discover useful global patterns.
        if n_low in (512, 1024):
            fa, fsc = fourier_search(
                n_low,
                rng,
                max_cycles=(3 if n_low <= 512 else 2),
                deadline=max(deadline, low_deadline - 20.0)
            )
            if fa is not None:
                best_a, best_sc = consider(fa, fsc, best_a, best_sc, f"fourier-n{n_low}")
                if fsc < low_best_sc:
                    low_best_sc = float(fsc)
                    low_best_a = fa.copy()

        n_refine = min(3, len(scored))
        for rank in range(n_refine):
            if time.monotonic() > low_deadline:
                break
            _, ac = scored[rank]
            for beta in (50.0, 200.0):
                if time.monotonic() > low_deadline:
                    break
                sc, ac = run_lbfgs(ac, beta, 3000, 30000, low_deadline)
                best_a, best_sc = consider(ac, sc, best_a, best_sc, f"low-lbfgs-n{n_low}-r{rank}-b{int(beta)}")
                if sc < low_best_sc:
                    low_best_sc = float(sc)
                    low_best_a = ac.copy()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # LP direction from a good low-resolution pattern
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    high_deadline = _T0 + 0.85 * work
    if low_best_a is not None and time.monotonic() < low_deadline - 5.0:
        try:
            f_low = resample_sequence(low_best_a, 512)
            g_dir = generate_lp_direction(f_low)
            if g_dir is not None and best_a is not None:
                target_len = len(best_a)
                g_hi = resample_sequence(g_dir, target_len)
                for tmix in (0.30, 0.60):
                    if time.monotonic() > high_deadline - 30.0:
                        break
                    b = (1.0 - tmix) * normalize_sum(best_a, 1.0)
                    b += tmix * normalize_sum(g_hi, 1.0)
                    b = normalize_sum(np.clip(b, 0.0, 1000.0), 1.0)
                    sc, b_opt = run_lbfgs(b, 350.0, 3500, 35000, high_deadline)
                    best_a, best_sc = consider(b_opt, sc, best_a, best_sc, f"LP-t{tmix:.2f}")
        except Exception:
            pass

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 2: higher-resolution L-BFGS continuation
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if parent is None:
        lengths = [4096, 8192, 16384]
    else:
        p_len = int(np.clip(parent.size, 4096, 16384))
        lengths = [p_len]
        for n in (8192, 16384):
            if n not in lengths:
                lengths.append(n)

    # Remove duplicates but preserve parent-first ordering.
    seen = set()
    ordered_lengths = []
    for n in lengths:
        n = int(n)
        if n not in seen:
            seen.add(n)
            ordered_lengths.append(n)

    for n in ordered_lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > high_deadline:
            break
        if n < 256 or n > 16384:
            continue

        if n <= 8192:
            max_inits = 12
            max_cand = 4
        else:
            max_inits = 8
            max_cand = 3

        inits = build_candidate_inits(n, rng, parent, best_a, max_inits=max_inits)
        scored = score_inits(inits)
        if not scored:
            continue

        if n <= 2048:
            betas = [30.0, 100.0, 300.0, 800.0, 2000.0]
            cap_iter, cap_fun = 8000, 80000
        elif n <= 8192:
            betas = [40.0, 120.0, 350.0, 1000.0, 2500.0]
            cap_iter, cap_fun = 4500, 45000
        else:
            betas = [50.0, 150.0, 500.0, 1500.0]
            cap_iter, cap_fun = 3000, 30000

        for rank in range(min(max_cand, len(scored))):
            if time.monotonic() > high_deadline:
                break
            ac = scored[rank][1]
            for beta in betas:
                if time.monotonic() > high_deadline:
                    break
                sc, ac = run_lbfgs(ac, beta, cap_iter, cap_fun, high_deadline)
                best_a, best_sc = consider(ac, sc, best_a, best_sc, f"hi-n{n}-r{rank}-b{int(beta)}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 3: controlled extension to larger lengths, if it helps
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    ext_deadline = deadline
    if best_a is not None:
        for target in (8192, 16384):
            if time.monotonic() > ext_deadline:
                break
            if target <= len(best_a):
                continue
            for beta in (500.0, 1500.0):
                if time.monotonic() > ext_deadline:
                    break
                ac = resample_sequence(best_a, target)
                sc, ac = run_lbfgs(ac, beta, 4000, 40000, ext_deadline)
                best_a, best_sc = consider(ac, sc, best_a, best_sc, f"ext-n{target}-b{int(beta)}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 4: very high-beta final refinement
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if best_a is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
        for beta in (1000.0, 3000.0, 6000.0):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 20.0:
                break
            sc, ac = run_lbfgs(best_a, beta, 3000, 30000, deadline)
            best_a, best_sc = consider(ac, sc, best_a, best_sc, f"final-lbfgs-b{int(beta)}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 5: exact line search / local perturbations
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if best_a is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 250.0:
        sc, ac = gradient_line_search(best_a, 2500.0, max_evals=40, deadline=max(deadline - 120.0, time.monotonic()))
        best_a, best_sc = consider(ac, sc, best_a, best_sc, "grad-line-search")

    if best_a is not None:
        trial = 0
        while time.monotonic() < deadline - 90.0:
            if trial >= 6:
                break
            strategy = trial % 4
            ap = make_perturbed(best_a, rng, strategy)
            sc, ap = run_lbfgs(ap, 1000.0, 2500, 25000, deadline)
            best_a, best_sc = consider(ap, sc, best_a, best_sc, f"local-{trial}-s{strategy}")
            trial += 1

    if best_a is None:
        best_a = np.full(1024, 1.0 / 1024.0, dtype=np.float64)
        best_sc = exact_score(best_a)

    # Make sure the returned sequence has a safe scale for the evaluator.
    best_a = normalize_sum(np.clip(best_a, 0.0, 1000.0), 1.0)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Final selection: optionally verify a few candidates with the external
    # evaluator if it is fast enough. Do not rely on it during the search.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    final_list = [float(min(1000.0, max(0.0, x))) for x in best_a]
    if sum(final_list) < 0.01:
        final_list = [0.01] + [0.0] * (len(final_list) - 1)

    chosen = final_list
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 45.0 and len(final_list) <= 12000:
        candidates = [final_list]
        if parent is not None and parent.size != len(final_list):
            candidates.append([float(min(1000.0, max(0.0, x))) for x in
                               normalize_sum(resample_sequence(parent, len(final_list)), 1.0)])
        if low_best_a is not None and len(low_best_a) != len(final_list):
            candidates.append([float(min(1000.0, max(0.0, x))) for x in
                               normalize_sum(resample_sequence(low_best_a, len(final_list)), 1.0)])

        best_external = np.inf
        best_external_list = None
        for cand in candidates[:3]:
            out = [float(min(1000.0, max(0.0, x))) for x in cand]
            if sum(out) < 0.01:
                out = [0.01] + [0.0] * (len(out) - 1)
            try:
                ev = float(evaluate_sequence(out))
                print(f"[verify] external={ev:.10f} n={len(out)}")
                if np.isfinite(ev) and ev < best_external:
                    best_external = ev
                    best_external_list = out
            except Exception:
                pass

        if best_external_list is not None:
            chosen = best_external_list

    print(f"[final] internal_best={best_sc:.10f} n={len(best_a)} used_time={time.monotonic() - _T0:.1f}s")
    return chosen
```
